5 iPhone features you should be using after updating to iOS 27

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

iOS 27 just made your iPhone a lot more capable.

The iPhone is easy to recommend to anyone looking for great smartphone hardware and long-term software support. The previous few iOS releases, however, have been pretty janky. With iOS 27, Apple therefore shifted its focus to ironing out bugs and improving overall stability rather than cramming in another round of flashy new features, and a good chunk of the WWDC 2026 keynote covered improvements such as snappier app launch times, faster AirDrop transfers and better memory management.

Apple's commitment to software updates also means iOS 27 compatibility extends all the way back to the iPhone 11, which is great news since iOS 26 would have been a terrible final major update to be stuck on. If you haven't already updated your iPhone, you can do so by navigating to Settings > General > Software Update.

Not much has changed visually, which makes sense since Apple only recently overhauled its design language with Liquid Glass. There is now a system-wide transparency slider, though, which many users had been asking about. Reports about battery life are mixed — some say they're getting less screen-on time than before, while others haven't had issues. Personally, iOS 27 has been such a blessing performance-wise on my aging iPhone 15 Pro Max that I'm willing to put up with slightly worse battery life.

Of course, stability upgrades aren't the only thing Apple introduced with iOS 27. The update also brings a handful of useful tidbits, and here are five features you should definitely check out after updating your iPhone.

Apple Intelligence has had a rocky start. After all, it took Apple two whole iOS generations to deliver on its promises of a more capable Siri. Most of the Apple Intelligence suite has been pretty underwhelming, unless you're one of the five people who enjoy generating custom emojis using Genmoji, but iOS 27 changes that. The Shortcuts app has received a major upgrade, and Apple Intelligence might finally have a genuinely useful feature to show for itself.

You've been able to create pretty complex shortcuts and automations on iOS for a while now, and you can even import shortcuts that others have created and shared. iOS 27 now lets you create shortcuts using natural language prompts, which Apple Intelligence handles. When you launch the app and tap the + button, you can start describing the shortcut you want.

In my testing, the integration works pretty well and usually creates straightforward shortcuts without issues. That said, it pieces together the wrong actions sometimes and ends up with a shortcut that doesn't do exactly what you want. Fortunately, you can still manually tweak shortcuts created using Apple Intelligence by tapping Edit.

You can also use Apple Intelligence actions within your shortcuts. Apple currently offers three AI models, including an on-device model for faster processing. You can create shortcuts for actions involving information extraction, summarization or generation of text and documents. The Cloud and Cloud Pro models do have a daily rate limit, though.

The Wallet app on the iPhone stores your payment cards, transit passes, event and cinema tickets, digital keys and more. Until now, users could only add digital passes and credentials if the issuer supported Apple Wallet and provided a compatible pass. While several companies and services offered this convenience, plenty of issuers didn't support it. As a collector, I was a bit disheartened to see my local IMAX theater stop offering Apple Wallet passes for movie tickets.

With iOS 27, you can create custom passes and add them to Apple Wallet. This is extremely handy for those with loyalty cards or gym memberships that don't offer native Apple Wallet support. There are two ways you can create a pass in Apple Wallet, and Apple Intelligence comes in handy again here. On supported iPhone models, you can open an image (or screenshot) of a ticket or membership card with a barcode, swipe up and tap on Add Pass to Wallet.

If this option doesn't show up, you can also open the Wallet app, tap the + icon at the top and select Create a Pass. Tap on Continue and take a photo of the ticket or ID card. The Wallet app will then use Visual Intelligence to extract relevant information from the image and create a pass. If you want to change the pass' details or visuals, tap Edit.

For years, Apple's virtual assistant, Siri, has lagged behind competitors like Google Assistant. Slow responses are forgivable, but Siri would often struggle with even the most basic requests. Apple first promised a major Siri overhaul with iOS 18's Apple Intelligence suite, but it's taken over two years to come to fruition. iOS 27 comes with Siri AI, and users with a compatible iPhone can join the waitlist by navigating to Settings > Siri. The AI-powered virtual assistant is still in beta, but things are already starting to look promising.

Siri now understands and fulfills your requests noticeably better, with a huge improvement in how it handles follow-up queries. Though it still takes a hot second to process most requests, its responses are more relevant, and it doesn't hit you with the "Here's what I found on the web" reply as often. What's more impressive about Siri AI, though, is the fact that it now understands personal context.

It can pull information from your messages, emails, photos and more to answer questions or perform tasks that previously required you to manually dig through different apps. Third-party app support is still limited, but if you mainly use iMessage and the default Mail app on your iPhone, you'll likely find Siri to be one of iOS 27's biggest improvements. The Camera app has a new Siri mode as well, which taps into Visual Intelligence to answer questions about whatever you're pointing your camera at.

The AirPods are some of the most popular wireless earbuds on the market, and they work best when paired with an iPhone, iPad or Mac. Most modern AirPods models support Spatial Audio with head tracking, and several music streaming services like Apple Music and Tidal let you enjoy Dolby Atmos content. Sound quality is also widely regarded as one of their strongest selling points, particularly on the higher-end Pro models like the AirPods Pro 3 that we reviewed. With iOS 27, though, you can finally tweak the sound to your liking using a built-in equalizer.

Apps like Apple Music have long allowed you to apply EQ presets, but the new AirPods equalizer works system-wide. The feature is available on AirPods 4 and newer, AirPods Pro 2 and newer and the AirPods Max 2. Connect your AirPods to your iPhone, launch the Settings app, tap on the AirPods settings at the top of the screen and navigate to Audio & Routing > Equalizer.

The Recommended option is selected by default, which delivers the balanced sound profile you've grown used to. If you tap on Custom, you can manually adjust the lows, mids and highs to your liking. If you prefer thumpy bass, increase the lows slider, but if you want vocals to sound clearer, try bumping up the mids or highs instead. Though Apple is only offering a 3-band equalizer, it's still a welcome addition. My AirPods Pro 2 already deliver punchy lows, but as a basshead, I appreciate having the option to crank up the bass even further.

Apple's latest mobile OS also has several additions that make browsing more convenient. For starters, Safari now lets you set up alerts for webpage changes, so you don't have to check yourself. This is useful if you're tracking a product's price, waiting for tickets to go on sale or keeping an eye on when an out-of-stock item becomes available again.

On your iPhone, launch Safari, head to a webpage you want to track, tap on the icon with three horizontal lines in the address bar and select Notify Me. Since this feature uses Apple Intelligence, you can describe what exactly you want Safari to monitor. You can then choose how frequently Safari checks the webpage for changes.

Safari also uses Apple Intelligence to organize your active tabs into relevant categories. For instance, if you have a couple of tabs open for an upcoming event, Safari can group tabs related to ticket purchases, venue directions or parking information into a single category. To set the feature up, enter the tab overview in Safari, tap on the sort button and select the Automatically Create Topics option.

Apple Intelligence also lets you create custom Safari extensions. Tap on the icon with the three horizontal lines in Safari and select Describe Extension. Apple lists a few examples, but you can ask Apple Intelligence to build an extension that performs a specific task. The few extensions I tried creating were surprisingly good at doing what I asked, though your mileage may vary.

The Case For Pre-Breach Intelligence: Why Buying Access Before Attackers Do Beats Cleaning Up

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Austin Berglas is Managing Director and Head of Cyber Operations at Nardello & Co. and the former head of FBI New York Cyber.

​For decades, enterprise security spending has followed one pattern: Detect the intrusion, contain it, investigate and recover. The breach occurs, and the security team responds as fast as it can.

That approach is expensive and increasingly avoidable.

IBM’s 2025 Cost of a Data Breach Report put the global average cost of a breach at $4.44 million and the average time to identify and contain at 241 days. Yes, this was a nine-year low, yet it still amounts to nearly eight months in which an attacker can sit inside a network, expand access and live off the land to conduct a larger-scale attack.

This is the limitation of post-breach incident response: It’s reactive. A SIEM can only correlate logs that exist, and an EDR platform can only flag malware after it’s been deployed. The industry has put enormous effort into faster detection and response and far less effort into stopping the incident from happening in the first place.

Most modern intrusions don’t begin with a zero-day exploit or custom malware. They begin with a transaction. Initial access brokers are criminals who specialize in breaching a network and selling that foothold to the highest bidder. These brokers supply a large share of the initial access used by ransomware crews and other individuals and groups conducting cyber-enabled crimes.

A gbhackers report noted that ​Rapid7 researchers tracked initial access listings across five major underground forums and found the average base price “rose from $2,726 in 2024 to $113,275 in the second half of 2025.” Additionally, Rapid7’s earlier dataset showed that more than 70% of listings “included some degree of privilege,” which means buyers were getting more than just a low-level login—they were purchasing a foothold from which to move laterally and expand access.​​​​​

Once access is listed, it doesn’t sit there for long. KELA Cyber research from 2022 found that access was often sold within one to three days of listing and that victims were listed on ransomware leak sites an average of 23 to 36 days later. This is a fairly significant window to conduct an attack.​

By the time the tech stack flags unusual activity, the access has already changed hands on a criminal forum. The attack that eventually triggers an alert is frequently the second or third step in a chain that began with a transaction on some dark web forum, completely unknown to the security team.

Pre-breach intelligence inverts the model. Instead of waiting for, and reacting to, a compromise, it involves direct monitoring of and, in some cases, legally acquiring access to the criminal marketplaces where access and credentials are bought and sold. In this way, an organization can learn if access to its own network is for sale before an attacker completes the purchase and exploits it.​

This changes what “detection” means. Pre-breach intelligence tells you that a specific method of access to your organization’s network is actively being marketed to buyers who intend to use it for a variety of crimes, such as ransomware, fraud or corporate espionage, while there’s still time to rotate credentials, patch the stolen access route and notify law enforcement before anything nefarious happens.​

Pre-breach intelligence is also significant for third-party risk. Vendors and partners are often the weakest link in an organization’s security posture and are frequently the last to know when their own access has been compromised. Monitoring the marketplaces where access to those vendors is sold can surface a third-party compromise weeks before the vendor discloses it or it becomes public.

None of this replaces traditional security operations and best practices. Endpoint detection, patch management and a competent incident response plan are still nonnegotiable. Pre-breach intelligence adds a layer that sits earlier in the attack chain. This is the difference between finding out your house key was copied and finding out someone already used it to get inside.

Insurers have started to take the approach seriously. In March 2025, Reinsurance News reported that AXA XL’s specialty risk division had partnered with DarkwebIQ, a pre-attack intelligence provider that intercepts access sales by infiltrating forums, marketplaces and private channels.​

Post-breach response has dominated budgets because its value is easy to explain. Here was the incident, here’s the cost and here’s what we spent fixing it. Prevention is a harder sell internally because success looks like nothing is happening. However, given how long it can take to remediate and recover from a data breach, and the significant cost associated, nothing happening is exactly the outcome worth paying for.

An organization that intercepts access before an attacker uses it avoids the costs that follow a breach. This can include months of containment, legal fees, incident response engagements, remediation and recovery, regulatory exposure and reputational damage.

Security leaders should ask whether access to their network is for sale today. If their current tools, vendors or partners can’t answer that, there’s no question that they’ll learn about their exposure on the attacker’s timeline rather than their own.​

Forbes Technology Council is an invitation-only community for world-class CIOs, CTOs and technology executives. Do I qualify?

Election Day is unfolding across America after a surge in early voting for the midterms

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The final day of voting in this year’s midterm elections arrived Tuesday against a backdrop of uncertainty after a year in which President Donald Trump has tried to interfere in how states run their elections and sown doubts about the outcome should Republicans lose.

Millions of Americans already have cast their ballots, with early voting turnout on pace to eclipse that of the last midterm elections. In contrast to the president’s continued rhetoric about election cheating, voting so far has unfolded with relatively few problems.

Trump has spent months trying to use the power of the presidency to put his thumb on the scale of this year’s elections, which will determine whether his party keeps control of Congress.

His Justice and Homeland Security departments have been involved in a massive hunt for voters who are not U.S. citizens, at one point engaging in a made-for-the-cameras predawn operation that led to more than two dozen arrests in California. The Department of Justice was planning its largest-ever effort to monitor Election Day polling places, countered by an even larger effort among Democrats and allied groups, and at the president’s urging had sued to block certification of Alaska’s results if the outcome of the Senate race favored Democrats.

At the same time, Trump’s constant false claims about widespread voter fraud led election officials and Democratic groups across the country to plan for the possibility of immigration agents at voting sites or federal officers seizing voting machines. His administration has insisted that no armed federal officials would be guarding the polls on Election Day.

Courts have blocked the Trump administration’s efforts to influence elections

In the run-up to Tuesday, none of the steps Democrats most feared had come to pass, and the Republican administration’s legal maneuvers had been largely blocked by the courts.

One of the Trump administration’s most high-profile attempts to interfere in state elections was turned away Monday night by the U.S. Supreme Court, which rejected its push to force California to set aside thousands of ballots as part of its search for noncitizen voters. In another Monday night ruling, a federal judge said the Trump administration must stop sending voter lists of alleged noncitizens to election officials.

Even the administration’s own record of arrests and charges has demonstrated what research and state investigations have made clear for years — that instances of noncitizens casting ballots are extremely rare.

Monday’s rulings followed another defeat for the president at the Supreme Court less than two months ago, when it rejected his attempt to limit mail balloting through changes at the Postal Service. That directive was included in one of two executive orders Trump has signed during his second term that attempted to federalize certain aspects of elections, even though the Constitution gives the states, and sometimes Congress, the authority to set election rules.

Voting begins smoothly with some weather disruptions in the Southwest

The first polling sites opened at 6 a.m. EST Tuesday in a handful of states — Connecticut, Indiana, Kentucky, Louisiana, Maine, Missouri, New Jersey, New York and Virginia.

Heavy rains and flash flooding in a swath of the Southwest were expected to make it harder for some voters to reach polling places in parts of Colorado, Arizona, Utah and New Mexico. Election administrators in the Navajo Nation announced changes to several of its polling locations, and in Utah’s rural San Juan County, voters were being allowed to vote electronically on Tuesday because some of them may not be able to reach polling places.

Election administrators and voting rights advocates have been advising voters casting late absentee ballots to use drop boxes or deliver them to polling places in person rather than mail them. The advice came after concerns emerged that recent changes in U.S. Postal Service operations could result in postmarking delays that would mean mail ballots could not be counted. Late-arriving ballots or late postmarks disenfranchised tens of thousands of voters in California, Oregon and Washington, which mail ballots to all voters, during this year’s primaries.

Trump hasn’t committed to accepting the election results

The most significant disruptions ahead of Election Day were the Trump administration’s attempts to interfere. Voting rights groups, Democrats and the Justice Department have been exchanging a flurry of legal filings over alleged noncitizen voters, federal election monitors, federal intimidation of election officials and other concerns. They reveal how Trump’s attempts to expand federal control over state-run elections — combined with high-stakes races — are resulting in court battles that could extend past Election Day into ballot counting and certification of the results.

Election law experts said Trump’s lawsuits go beyond historic norms, but they said the administration’s legal arguments were unlikely to convince the courts.

Trump also has ramped up his own equivocations about whether the election will be legitimate, as he has in the past. A week before Election Day, when a reporter asked him if he would accept the results, he offered a qualified answer.

“I will if they are honest,” he said.

Associated Press writer Geoff Mulvihill contributed to this report.

—Ali Swenson, Associated Press

2026 Full Moon calendar: When to see the Full Moon and phases

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The phenomenon of a Full Moon arises when our planet, Earth, is precisely sandwiched between the Sun and the Moon. This alignment ensures the entire side of the Moon that faces us gleams under sunlight. Thanks to the Moon’s orbit around Earth, the angle of sunlight hitting the lunar surface and being reflected back to our planet changes. That creates different lunar phases.

The next Full Moon will be the November Beaver Moon, which occurs at 9:54 a.m. EST on Tuesday, Nov. 24, 2026. It will also be a Super Moon.

We’ll update this article multiple times each week with the latest moonrise, moonset, Full Moon schedule, and some of what you can see in the sky each week.

Here’s the complete list of Full Moons this year and their traditional names.

(All times Eastern; * denotes a Super Moon.)

The images below show the day-by-day phases of the Moon in November. The Full Moon this month will be on Tuesday, Nov. 24, 2026.

The times below for sunrise, sunset, moonrise, and moonset are given in local time from 40° N 90° W. The Moon’s illumination is given at 10 P.M. local time from the same location. All other times are Eastern unless otherwise specified. For more details on upcoming sky events, check out our full Sky This Week column.

Tuesday, November 3The Moon now passes 0.8° south of the bright star Regulus in Leo at 4 A.M. EST. Some regions of the world will see our satellite pass in front of Regulus in an occultation, but again, the event is not visible from North America.

The pair rises together shortly after midnight on the 2nd. By 5 A.M. local time on the 3rd, the Moon-star pair is nearly 50° high in the southeast. The waning Moon has shrunk from roughly 43% lit yesterday morning to 33% lit today. It is just under a week from New phase, which occurs on the 9th.

Sunrise: 6:31 A.M.Sunset: 4:55 P.M.Moonrise: 12:34 A.M.Moonset: 2:07 P.M.Moon Phase: Waning crescent (26%)

Wednesday, November 4Venus sits 2° south of Virgo’s brightest star, Spica, in the early-morning sky. The pair is joined by a lovely crescent Moon just over 20% lit, high above them in Leo.

Sunrise: 6:32 A.M.Sunset: 4:54 P.M.Moonrise: 1:44 A.M.Moonset: 2:30 P.M.Moon Phase: Waning crescent (17%)

The phases of the Moon are: New Moon, waxing crescent, First Quarter, waxing gibbous, Full Moon, waning gibbous, Last Quarter, and waning crescent. A cycle starting from one Full Moon to its next counterpart, termed the synodic month or lunar month, lasts about 29.5 days.

Though a Full Moon only occurs during the exact moment when Earth, Moon, and Sun form a perfect alignment, to our eyes, the Moon seems Full for around three days.

There are a wide variety of specialized names used to identify distinct types or timings of Full Moons. These names primarily trace back to a blend of cultural, agricultural, and natural observations about the Moon, aimed at allowing humans to not only predict seasonal changes, but also track the passage of time.

For instance, almost every month’s Full Moon boasts a name sourced from Native American, Colonial American, or other North American traditions, with their titles mirroring seasonal shifts and nature’s events.

Wolf Moon (January): Inspired by the cries of hungry wolves.

Snow Moon (February): A nod to the month’s often heavy snowfall.

Worm Moon (March): Named after the earthworms that signal thawing grounds.

Pink Moon (April): In honor of the blossoming pink wildflowers.

Flower Moon (May): Celebrating the bloom of flowers.

Strawberry Moon (June): Marks the prime strawberry harvest season.

Buck Moon (July): Recognizing the new antlers on bucks.

Sturgeon Moon (August): Named after the abundant sturgeon fish.

Corn Moon (September): Signifying the corn harvesting period.

Hunter’s Moon (October): Commemorating the hunting season preceding winter.

Beaver Moon (November): Reflects the time when beavers are busy building their winter dams.

Cold Moon (December): Evocative of winter’s chill.

In addition, there are a few additional names for Full Moons that commonly make their way into public conversations and news.

Super Moon: This term is reserved for a Full Moon that aligns with the lunar perigee, which is the Moon’s nearest point to Earth in its orbit. This proximity renders the Full Moon unusually large and luminous. For a Full Moon to earn the Super Moon tag, it should be within approximately 90 percent of its closest distance to Earth.

Blue Moon: A Blue Moon is the second Full Moon in a month that experiences two Full Moons. This phenomenon graces our skies roughly every 2.7 years. Though the term suggests a color, Blue Moons aren’t truly blue. Very occasionally, atmospheric conditions such as recent volcanic eruptions might lend the Moon a slightly blueish tint, but this hue isn’t tied to the term.

Black Moon: A Black Moon is a recent, unofficial term that can describe one of two situations. Either the third of four New Moons in a single astronomical season or the second of two New Moons in a month is a Black Moon. As its name suggests, the Black Moon is completely dark, as it is a New Moon that occurs when the Moon is between the Sun and Earth. The New Moon cannot be observed except during a total solar eclipse.

Harvest Moon: Occurring closest to the autumnal equinox, typically in September, the Harvest Moon is often renowned for a distinct orange tint it might display. This Full Moon rises close to sunset and sets near sunrise, providing extended hours of bright moonlight. Historically, this was invaluable to farmers gathering their produce.

What is the difference between a Full Moon and a New Moon? A Full Moon is witnessed when Earth is between the Sun and the Moon, making the entire Moon’s face visible. Conversely, during a New Moon, the Moon lies between Earth and the Sun, shrouding its Earth-facing side in darkness.

How does the Full Moon influence tides? The Moon’s gravitational tug causes Earth’s waters to bulge, birthing tides. During both Full and New Moons, the Sun, Earth, and Moon are in alignment, generating “spring tides.” These tides can swing exceptionally high or low due to the combined gravitational influences of the Sun and Moon.

Yep, this version of the lizard is based on the Monsterverse franchise rather than the original Toho design or the more recent Minus Zero movie – as evidenced by a screenshot from King of the Monsters being featured on the back of the box.

While the aforementioned leaks got a mixed reception (with some fans being disappointed by the end result), I'm quite fond of the finished look. Those spines are such a bright blue that they sell the illusion they're glowing, for example, and it helps what could otherwise have been an indistinct lump of gray plastic pop. Did I prefer the more bestial look of the original community design? Yes. But the more I see of this, the more I like it.

Lego Godzilla will officially stride onto shelves this November 27 – though I sincerely doubt it'll take part in the Black Friday Lego deals that are sure to be appearing around then.

Despite the tensions of war, ex-Barclays CEO Bob Diamond is long Gulf. Here’s why

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“The region isn’t hiding in their bunker; they’re doing business,” Bob Diamond tells me on a call from Bahrain, where the Gateway Gulf investment conference is taking place. He is talking to Fortune against a backdrop of simmering tensions around the U.S. midterms and the possibility that the President may restart attacks on Iran.

“I don’t want to be dismissive, there is a war, there is a conflict, whatever term you want to use,” he said. “It’s real, and I’m not going to be dismissive of the impact that’s having on the UAE, on Bahrain, on Qatar. But I will tell you, if you take the time to get here and you’re on the ground, it’s business as usual. The kids are in school. People are working from the office. They’re not hiding at home. They’re doing business. They’re investing.”

The former CEO of Barclays is in Bahrain to announce that the U.K.-based advisory firm, Panmure Liberum, is opening its first regional office in the kingdom. Diamond’s investment vehicle, Atlas Merchant Capital, bought Panmure Gordon for £15.5m ($21m) in 2017. It agreed an all-share merger with Liberum in 2024.

Panmure Liberum is now one of the biggest advisors to U.K.-listed companies based on the number of clients, which total more than 250. It specializes in mid-market companies, with valuations below those served by the bulge-bracket investment banks such as Morgan Stanley and Goldman Sachs.

Panmure’s move to Bahrain will allow it to serve the whole region and increase the deal-flow for the advisory firm. Activity levels in the Gulf are in sharp contrast to the number of deals announced in the U.K. In the first half of 2026, deals in the U.K. were down 13% compared with the same period a year ago, according to PwC, a consultancy.

“You have all the talent in the world in London. It’s still a very attractive place for a lot of people,” Diamond said. “The skill set of advisory is very, very deep. But there’s not a lot of deals. I think the statistics on the number of companies that have gone public or raised equity is paltry compared to what it used to be, and they’re losing out not to Frankfurt or Dublin, but to the U.S. and, more and more, to the Middle East.”

This creates an opportunity for firms keen to expand.

“It means that we can build an advisory business here in Bahrain to serve middle market and family-owned businesses, which are dying for cover,” he said. “And we can then expand that to the Gulf Cooperation Council.”

“You have all the talent in the world in London, but not a lot of deals. Companies going public or raising equity are paltry compared to what they used to be, and they’re losing out to the U.S. and more, the Middle East”

As one of the smaller kingdoms in the region, Bahrain, says it is able to offer agility and clear and simple regulatory rules to investors. At Gateway Gulf, Mastercard announced an expansion of its deal with the Bahrain-based fintech firm, Infinios, to facilitate business-to-business travel payments. Olayan Kimberly-Clark, the partnership between Saudi Arabia and the tissue paper production giant, announced that it would be expanding production at its Bahrain paper mill.

“It’s a highly regulated banking sector,” Diamond said about why the kingdom was attractive. “Great banks want great regulation. It’s also a gateway, to steal a phrase from the conference, to the GCC. So all of a sudden, Panmure, which is a U.K.-focused advisory business, is now U.K. and GCC, so we’re very happy with it.”

Diamond said that there were also opportunities for Bahrain and the rest of the Gulf to develop stablecoin payment systems, one of the fastest ways to reduce the cost of doing business. As a digital currency pegged to the dollar, it is seen as a more secure way to settle transactions.

“Tokenization is just digitizing assets,” he said. “It’s making them more efficient. All of that relies on blockchain, which is an underpinning of 24/7 instantaneous settlement, a permanent record of every transaction. So, the regulators love it, at a fraction of the cost. It is going to be the future underpinning [for finance]. It’s infrastructure.”

Quick note: Companies are facing growing pressure to demonstrate returns on every investment while continuing to build for the future. Police open investigation into death of footballer after on-field collision

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Jesus de Miguel died in hospital as a result of injuries sustained while playing for Tenerife against FC Andorra in the Spanish second tier on Monday

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

From reproductive rights to climate change to Big Tech, The Independent is on the ground when the story is developing. Whether it's investigating the financials of Elon Musk's pro-Trump PAC or producing our latest documentary, 'The A Word', which shines a light on the American women fighting for reproductive rights, we know how important it is to parse out the facts from the messaging.

At such a critical moment in US history, we need reporters on the ground. Your donation allows us to keep sending journalists to speak to both sides of the story.

The Independent is trusted by Americans across the entire political spectrum. And unlike many other quality news outlets, we choose not to lock Americans out of our reporting and analysis with paywalls. We believe quality journalism should be available to everyone, paid for by those who can afford it.

Police in Spain have confirmed they have opened a “routine” investigation into possible involuntary manslaughter following the death of Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel, who died in hospital on Tuesday after suffering a cardiac arrest as a result of a collision during a match on Monday.

De Miguel, 30, collapsed on the pitch after a clash with Andorra goalkeeper Jesus Owono, who had rushed out of his area to clear the ball but struck the forward in the chest. The match was then abandoned and Owono was shown a red card following the incident, which took place in stoppage time of the second division fixture.

De Miguel was taken to hospital after receiving treatment on the pitch. Tenerife initially reported that he was “stable, despite the severity of his condition” before reporting that his neurological condition had worsened in the early hours of Tuesday. They confirmed his death at around 8am on Tuesday.

While Spain’s National Police said prosecutors would be involved, authorities in the Canary Islands said the opening of an investigation into the footballer’s death was a routine procedure. Under Spanish law, any death that is deemed non-natural automatically triggers a judicial inquiry.

In a statement, Tenerife said: “Heartbroken, with a lump in our throats and unable to believe this is true, we at CD Tenerife announce the tragic passing of Jesus de Miguel Alameda. There are no words of comfort. There is absolutely nothing that can ease this overwhelming sense of sadness.”

Owono, a Equatorial Guinea international, has deactivated his Instagram account since the incident. A statement from Andorra read: “With broken hearts, we want to send our most sincere condolences to the family of Jesus de Miguel, to all his teammates, friends, and to CD Tenerife. Rest in peace.”

Several Spanish teams, including Barcelona and Real Madrid, paid tribute, while Fifpro, the world players’ union, said: “Fifpro is deeply saddened by the passing of CD Tenerife player Jesus de Miguel. Our thoughts are with Jesus’ family, friends, team-mates, everyone at CD Tenerife, and all those affected. Rest in peace, Jesus.”

La Liga president Javier Tebas expressed his shock and said the league would work to support the club and De Miguel’s family, and said he would speak to Tenerife about potentially postponing their next match out of concern for the players’ welfare. Speaking in an interview with Television Canaria, Tebas also raised the possibility of a joint tribute between La Liga and the Segunda Division.

Uefa president Aleksander Ceferin said: “We are heartbroken by the death of Jesus de Miguel Alameda. We have searched for words and found only silence. In that silence, our hearts and thoughts are with his family, his team-mates, everyone at Tenerife, and all those who mourn this tragic loss.”

All Uefa matches this week will be preceded with a moment of silence in memory of De Miguel while the Spanish Football Federation announced the postponed of Tuesday’s Copa del Rey and Copa del Reina matches. “We extend our deepest condolences to his family, friends and loved ones, as well as to Club Deportivo Tenerife and all its fans, whom we accompany in these moments of great sorrow,” it said.

I'm blown away by the new Bambu Lab H3C - is this the best 3D printer of all time?

A built-in AMS, 9 spools, filament drying, craft tools, multi-diameter prints, TPU-optimised, what else could you need?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Bambu Lab has just proven exactly why it remains the most innovative brand on the market when it comes to 3D printing. The all-new H3C pushes boundaries unlike any machine I've used before, blending an exquisite tool-swap design with multi-material compatibility, multi-diameter printing, options for processes like cutting and foil transfer, as well as filament storage and drying, all in one beast of a unit.

I honestly think Bambu Lab might have single-handedly mastered 3D printing with this mic drop of an announcement. I know that's a bold statement, but I can say with confidence, having tested the H3C for the past few weeks, that nothing else even comes close.

At this point, we might as well scrap our guide to the best 3D printers and just recommend the H3C from now on.

Jokes aside, every time the 3D printing community has raised an issue, Bambu has responded with a "hold my beer" like approach to fixing it. Purge waste? Eradicated. Wet filament? Not anymore. Long print times? Cut in half thanks to the H3C and Swaptek technology. I'm not sure what else can be improved.

Where to begin? The highlights boil down to the new Swaptek tool changer, comprising 9 PrintTools (nozzle + a PTFE tube), each with its own filament path for effortless and speedy colour changes in under 6 seconds.

Not to mention there's the new FlexFeed extruder, which accommodates both 1.75mm filament and 2.85mm filament for super soft TPU like the 75A Bambu Lab spools I've been testing.

The new AMS 3H is humongous, and has been superbly designed to not only store your filament but dry it too, with 2 dedicated chamber slots. Several of the spool slots also have what the company calls a bypass feed, so using materials that aren't suitable for AMS auto-loading, like TPU, has never been easier (I've got a video on this coming soon).

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

The only thing that the H3C is missing is a laser module, but I think that's for the best in this case and not needed for this kind of product. You can always get yourself a Bambu Lab R1 if you want to join the laser cutting club, or check out some of the best laser cutters and engravers out there from brands like xTool and Creality.

It seems like Bambu is on a mission to conquer the craft space, having launched its first standalone laser this year with the R1, and having recently announced that it's dipping its toe into 3D scanners as well. I'm calling it now: in 2027, there will be a Bambu Lab sticker maker, keyring machine, or some type of mini portable maker.

Forget Takeout: Customers Love These Frozen Mexican Foods

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you're lucky, you live just a short ride away from a half dozen quality Mexican restaurants serving bubbling bowls of pozole, punchy tacos, and sultry stews of slow-cooked meats. But not everyone lives in communities blessed with quality Mexican cooking. And that's a major bummer, because this regional cuisine is simply electric, balancing deep flavors with fresh herbs, warm spices, and piquant chiles. Mexican food plays with different textures and shapes, allowing you to compose bites that are simultaneously creamy and crisp, cool and spicy, savory and sweet.

Thankfully, you can enjoy decent Mexican and Tex-Mex-inspired food from the comfort of your couch. And all it takes is a trip to your local supermarket. First, let's preface this entire article by saying that a good restaurant will almost always outdo a frozen dupe. Still, there is something to be said for the convenience and cost savings of air-frying a chimichanga or microwaving a burrito for lunch. I turned to the internet to find frozen Mexican meals comparable to what you might get at a restaurant or fast-casual takeout spot. After sorting through hundreds of reviews and comments, I put together a list of the top customer-favorite Mexican appetizers, sides, and main meals available in the freezer aisle.

The foundation of a good tamale is corn, specifically corn that's been soaked in an alkaline solution (AKA nixtamalized), which transforms the dense starchy kernels into a pliable, dough-like carbohydrate called masa. Making masa (and tamales) requires time, skill, precision, and technique. But if you don't possess any of the above, then heating up a frozen tamale is your best option.

There are a surprising number of choices in this department, but customers are especially fond of the Trader Joe's versions. Like many Trader Joe's items, people gripe that these come in and out of stock with about as much predictability as a June weather forecast. However, the cheese and green chile and the chicken and cheese varieties are consistently the most reliable (and available). Tamales make a good side dish, especially if you want an alternative to potatoes or pasta, or, like one Redditor, you can steam one and bring it for lunch. A negative is that since they have to be reheated, the masa interior can get a tad dry. To remedy this, Trader Joe's shoppers recommend a side of salsa, hot sauce, guacamole, rice and beans, or a blanket of extra melted cheese.

As far as frozen Mexican entrées go, the burrito bowl can be controversial. Why? Unlike a tamale, chimichanga, or taquito, a burrito bowl doesn't require the same finesse, so customers generally expect a higher quality product. Surprisingly, many reliable brands struggle to execute what seems like a simple dish. But not Kevin's Natural Foods. If unfamiliar, Kevin's positions itself as a better-for-you frozen meal. Ingredients are clean, non-GMO, and gluten-free. These restrictions are far from a hindrance. On the contrary, the quality of Kevin's frozen meals, especially its burrito bowls, is top-notch.

At least, that's what the customers say. "Absolutely loaded with cheese, chicken in every bite, rice perfectly cooked, veggies still have a bit of a snap to them, and the chipotle sauce has a ton of flavor," one Redditor commented. Others shared a similar opinion. With Kevin's, there's a general appreciation for the quality and portion size. The brand doesn't skimp on proteins or portion size, and whatever ingredients are included are thoughtfully prepared — no mushy rice, sad veggies, or gloopy cheese. The result keeps customers happy.

Many culinary historians consider mole the national dish of Mexico. The sauce's dynamic flavors mirror the country's history: dark and complex, with an underlying sweetness and grounding depth. Mole requires dozens of Mexico's most prized indigenous ingredients — chocolate, chiles, nuts, seeds, spices — to create something that's, frankly, unmatched in any other cuisine.

Does Trader Joe's version capture that centuries of Mexican culinary tradition? That would be impossible. However, given its price and unbelievable convenience, it does a pretty good job of imitating the sweet-and-savory balance of a traditional mole. With just one package, you can make multiple meals. And while a traditional mole takes hours — if not days — to prepare, TJ's frozen chicken mole takes just minutes in the microwave.

The taste is bold, but the texture of the chicken — not overly mushy or stringy — makes it ideal for several applications. Customers say they use it to fill enchiladas and tacos, but you can use it for everything from pizza to nachos to an omelet.

Known for its better-for-you ingredient profile, Amy's has been supplying organic frozen entrées for decades. While some frozen tortilla-wrapped items suffer from a textural breakdown, the tortillas housing Amy's enchilada filling hold up well. All of Amy's frozen entrées are vegetarian-friendly, but where they lack in meat, they make up for it in texture, flavor, and composition. Amy's cheese enchiladas have been a hit with shoppers for decades, with the blend of cheeses, toasty corn tortillas, and savory tomato-based enchilada sauce, and they still earn online praise today.

"I find these to have an incredible depth of flavor for a frozen meal," began one Redditor's review. "I would be 100% happy if this was served to me in a restaurant." Others say that the product is the best in frozen food. And while there's no question that shoppers have enjoyed and appreciated the taste of Amy's foods for years, they are a bit disgruntled about the ever-increasing price point. And while that might be on the higher end of the price spectrum, anything in the $7 or $8 range for a frozen meal does seem a little steep. But they're still beloved, despite that.

The taquito was created as a thrifty way to use up leftover tortillas and filling. Little did its creators know that the fried, toasty snack would become widely beloved happy marriage of taste, form, and texture. Taquitos are almost always made from corn tortillas and filled with everything from chicken and cheese to beef, veggies, and beans. But the best part of a taquito is its tubular shape that's seemingly designed for dipping.

Frozen taquitos aren't new, and their basic composition hasn't changed much over the years. You'll find a plethora of options in the freezer aisle, but customers are most fond of El Monterey's Extra Crunchy Southwest Chicken variety. Fans say it's hard to eat just one... or two. Shoppers also love its unique exterior crust.

The only downside to El Monterey's taquitos is the quantity of filling. There's a collective sigh at how the interiors have grown increasingly barren over the years, an observation noticeable in the chicken taquitos, where the chicken and vegetables retain their individual shapes, unlike the beef and bean option, which is more of a paste. An easy solution is to prepare a few different dips — sour cream, guacamole, queso — to add extra moisture and unctuousness.

If a taquito and a burrito had a baby, it would be a chimichanga. It's deep-fried, which ups the richness but also gives the tortilla a crispy shell that's the ideal foil for any smooth refried bean or cheese filling. Deep frying at home doesn't require the same precision as making a chocolate soufflé, but it still means more work and cleanup than simply reheating on a hot frying pan. That's why a good frozen chimichanga is crucial if you suddenly get that itch for this fried Tex-Mex specialty. And, again, the El Monterey brand delivers.

Online, there was a lot of love for El Monterey, saying it has some of the best frozen burritos and chimichangas on the market. Unlike the taquitos, the chimichangas have a generous filling that, as one person put it, is comparable to the reference photo on the bag. The key to executing these properly is in the preparation. The chimichanga is inherently greasy, so a quick thaw in the microwave followed by a crisp-up on a hot pan usually does the trick. An air-fryer works, too. Some people just throw them in the deep fryer because, well, why not?

The burrito is the quintessential frozen Mexican food item, and over the decades, dozens — if not hundreds — of brands have battled each other for category supremacy. Many have faltered, but a handful have held their ground. Like Red's. Founded in 2009, Red's focuses on one thing: burritos. Sure, it'll try a breakfast sandwich or a "Snacker," but its product lineup is mostly just an array of tortilla-wrapped goodies.

Customers are big fans of the steak and cheddar burrito. While other cheaper, mass-market frozen burritos rely on mushy, indistinguishable fillings, Red's has made a name for itself by using higher-quality ingredients. Although shoppers are big fans of Red's entire line of burritos, they do gripe about the prices. Heating the burrito is another issue entirely, but one Red's expert has a foolproof method: Run it under water in the sink for a few seconds before microwaving. The extra moisture makes the tortilla a bit springier and more pliable. Wrapping it in a wet paper towel will have a similar effect.

The Mexican Style Street Corn Triangles from Tastiez are far from traditional Mexican cuisine. Instead, they are a frozen food product inspired by the classic Mexican street food esquites, which is like a bolder, saltier version of creamed corn. Another way to think of it is like elote, that yummy, cheese-dusted ear of grilled corn, stripped off the cob.

This product is a major departure from the original dish, but if you're craving an easy appetizer inspired by an authentic Mexican dish, it's not such a bad substitute. And, most importantly (at least for this article), customers have given these fried, corn-filled triangles their seal of approval. Whereas your average stuffed, fried snack oozes its insides after a hard bake or big bite, these stay pretty much intact. The crisp outside and gooey center make for a satisfying finger food, but it's the taste that brings it home, with reviewers gushing over its delicious street corn flavor. They come out best when deep-fried, but the air-fryer is an excellent alternative that still yields a crisp exterior.

Today, you can find the most elaborate culinary delights in your supermarket freezer. But while brands can execute everything from frozen kimbap to cioppino, many still struggle to nail a good frozen taco. What has customers complaining is the ratio of filling to shell: not enough of the former and too much of the latter, resulting in a product that's dry and brittle. Maybe tacos are difficult to seal, so manufacturers are forced to under fill? Whatever the reason, some brands miss the mark, while others, like Don Miguel, lead by example.

The Don Miguel Chicken Chipotle Mini Tacos have an active and passionate Reddit community that few items on this list can match. But why? What makes this 50-pack of frozen tacos from Costco so special? The price and crave-worthiness. However, while the consensus is generally positive, long-time customers have commented that the tacos are a bit spicier than they used to be. For this issue, the main workaround is a cooling condiment, like Greek yogurt or sour cream. Still, although people do love these things, it seems like the perfect mini taco has yet to be discovered.

The churro is one of the most globally recognized Mexican desserts. It's basically a twist or extruded wand of fried dough that's tossed in cinnamon sugar and served with, if you're lucky, a smooth chocolate sauce or caramel. Churros come in many shapes and sizes. Costco, famous for scaling everything to the max, sells a foot-long version in its food court for just $1.49. But if you want to scratch that churro itch at home, you can pick up a pack of Hola Churros.

No, these aren't as long as the ones you'll find in the food court, but some regular Costco customers think they're just smaller versions of the same make and model. But how do they taste? Overall, shoppers are pretty pleased. Are they as crisp and pleasantly oily as the ones you'll find at a street fair or Mexican restaurant? Probably not. But they do the job. The key is finding ways to deliver that extra punch of flavor. Although they come with individual packs of cinnamon sugar, some Redditors recommend drizzling chocolate on them. They're also good as a topping for an ice cream sundae. For heating, skip the microwave and go for the air-fryer or toaster oven. No matter how you make them, they are an unbelievably good value: around $14.49 for a box of 30.

When you see a chile relleno on the menu at a Mexican restaurant, it's worth getting. This dish is pretty special, integrating all the different tastes and flavors that make cuisine from this part of the world so interesting and seductive. It's essentially a cheese-stuffed pepper that's battered, fried, and served on top of a rich tomato sauce. It takes some work to prepare, but it's a real stunner when it hits the table.

Somehow the team at Trader Joe's has managed to capture all these dynamic tastes and textures in one convenient, easy-to-heat frozen package. The batter is tempura-style, somewhat spongy but not in a soggy way, providing heft and unctuousness to the otherwise bare, roasted pepper wrapper. The cheese is pleasantly rubbery, and the sauce is savory and delicious. On Reddit, customers are surprised by how good it tastes, even despite the soft, slightly mushy texture. Of all the foods on this list, this one had the most overwhelmingly positive score for flavor. The consensus is that you should reheat the peppers in the air-fryer, not the microwave. Overall, people think the flavor is fantastic, and for just $5.99, you really can't go wrong.

Although this brand of Trader Joe's frozen rice is called "Spanish-style," its flavor profile is decidedly Mexican, drawing on spices and ingredients indigenous to Central America. Tomato gives it its mellow orange color, and a savory spice blend and quick toasting add an umami undertone that makes it easy to pair with dishes from across the culinary landscape, from madras lentils to gyros.

On Reddit, Trader Joe's faithful say it's an excellent accompaniment to many of the store's frozen and refrigerated Mexican entrées, like the birria, mole, green chili and cheese tamales, and even the salad kits. Others use it as a quick and flavorful foundation to a dish. Its utility is what makes this product so special. With dozens of comments on one Reddit thread dedicated to the Spanish-style rice, you can find dozens of interesting and inventive use cases. And while it's marketed as a microwavable grain, some shoppers are convinced you get a better result when you toast it in a pan.

First, I identified some of the more popular Mexican dishes that come frozen. From there, I explored different brands, and using Reddit, Facebook, and other social media channels, I got a sense of which products performed best.

For example, the top taquito maker didn't necessarily deliver the best burrito. I wanted to present a variety, so I didn't repeat dishes. It was difficult to find the top flavor(s), so when there wasn't a clear consensus, I tended to go with the one with the most feedback and attention.

Why We’ve Been Looking at the Hotel Brands vs. OTAs War All Wrong

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

The hotel industry’s distribution war has an overlooked third party: the owner. New questions about AI, loyalty and customer acquisition could make that imbalance even more consequential.

Maybe we've been looking at the hotel distribution war all wrong, all along.

For years, we at Skift have chronicled the battle between hotel brands and online travel agencies. Hilton and Marriott want guests booking direct; Booking.com and Expedia want those reservations to come through their platforms. Every new technology, most recently AI agents, is assessed by which side it will help.

The fight is real. But the usual framing leaves out a party with a very different balance sheet: the hotel owner.

The owner pays the brand for access to its commercial system and may also pay an OTA when that platform delivers the guest. The brand and the OTA negotiate over the customer, but it is often the property that must absorb the cost of both relationships and still turn a profit.

Hilton’s October 27 results make the distinction clear. The company reported $815 million in third-quarter franchise and licensing revenue, a line that

Tags: hotel owners, hotels, OTAs, skift aviation forum, skift commerce summit

Skift provides the insights. You make the decisions.

Decision intelligence for travel leaders.

Skift argues the brand-versus-OTA distribution fight leaves out the party that absorbs the cost of both relationships: the hotel owner. Hilton's third-quarter results, reported October 27, showed $815 million in franchise and licensing revenue across a system of 8,683 franchised and licensed properties, 892 managed hotels, and 45 hotels in its ownership segment. Hilton's annual filing states franchise royalties are calculated as a percentage of gross room revenue, with separate program fees for marketing, reservations, technology, and Hilton Honors. In Skift's illustrative example, a $200 room sold through an OTA at a hypothetical 15% commission leaves the owner with a $30 acquisition charge while the royalty is still calculated on the full $200. Hilton's fee economics track revenue flowing through its network; the owner's economics depend on what is left after acquisition costs. MCR Hotels CEO Tyler Morse, who owns and operates 150 hotels including the unflagged TWA Hotel, has argued for years that owners should move away from OTAs and has described hotel ownership as a business of buying reservations. On Hilton's latest earnings call, CEO Chris Nassetta said AI should eventually bring more direct business at lower cost while OTAs remain important for a segment of the business, and cited the RISE system-fee cuts launched in June 2025 — 30 basis points off loyalty costs system-wide and another 50 basis points for hotels meeting a quality threshold, both reductions to system fees rather than royalty rates. Skift's analysis: if AI agents route bookings into brand systems, the owner's test is whether the new channel improves margin after every fee, and that data doesn't exist yet.

Ethereum Privacy Network Aztec Rolls Out Major Security and Speed Upgrade

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ethereum Privacy Network Aztec Rolls Out Major Security and Speed Upgrade

Aztec, the privacy-focused Ethereum layer-2 network, has rolled out a major upgrade that fixes a critical vulnerability from its previous version, doubles the speed of private transactions, and ushers in a wave of applications built to keep user data hidden.

The Aztec Foundation said Tuesday that the upgrade, called Aztec Tierra and designated Alpha V6, delivers stronger security, formal verification of its proving system and faster deposits from Ethereum, alongside a refreshed brand.

Aztec is a decentralized layer-2 with more than 3,000 validators and fully programmable private smart contracts, and it pitches itself as having no privileged viewing keys or centralized sequencer, meaning no single operator can peer into or control user activity.

The upgrade resolves known vulnerabilities from earlier versions, including a critical issue discovered in V5, and adds extensive hardening. Notably, that includes a new AI-powered security testing harness and formal verification of core cryptographic components, a mathematical process for proving code behaves as intended.

The move reflects a broader scramble across crypto to harden code as AI tools make vulnerabilities easier to find.

On performance, Aztec said private transactions now generate client-side proofs in about one second on a standard device, twice as fast as before, while a new architecture cuts the time for Ethereum-to-Aztec messages from an average of two minutes to as little as 12 seconds, speeding up deposits and cross-chain activity.

“Privacy shouldn’t be something you have to request or trust someone else to protect,” said Zac Williamson, co-founder and CEO of the Aztec Foundation. “It should be built into the network.”

The upgrade arrives with a crop of private applications spanning payments, trading, AI and social.

Leading them is zk.money, a private payments and savings app built by Aztec Labs that the team revived this year. With Tierra’s faster messaging, users can deposit directly from any Ethereum wallet without complex bridging.

Others in the lineup include Galactica for private AI inference, Nemi for private perpetuals trading, a private browser wallet called Nulo, and Rolling Chat, a private message board based on Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin’s own Aztec experiments.

The launch lands amid a renewed push for privacy on Ethereum. Buterin has laid out plans for a “cryptographic world computer” and pressed to slash the cost of quantum-safe privacy, while a separate team spun out a for-profit firm to drive institutional privacy adoption.

Aztec, which raised $17 million in an earlier funding round to build out its network, is betting that privacy becomes a default feature of Ethereum rather than an add-on.

The boss ofÂ Wardogs developer Bulkhead has toldÂ Edge magazine of his intentions to give the entire studio the whole month of December off work, following the successful early access launch of the tactical FPS. He’s also suggested that the players also “fuck off in December,” pointing out they’ll probably be busy playing GTA 6 instead.

Launched in September,Â Wardogs is a multiplayer FPS that allows 100 players to have fight one another across an enormous map, and it’s proven extremely popular. Hitting well over 400,000 concurrent players in its first few days, and still reaching over 100,000 simultaneous players a day, sales estimates suggest around five million copies. So if nothing else, the team can afford a good few weeks off.

Joe Brammer, the CEO of Bulkhead, isn’t exactly shy. Rather uncomfortably, soon after the launch of Wardogs, he said that the company doesn’t hire people known to complain about being forced to crunch. At the same time, in the new interview withÂ Edge (viaÂ GamesRadar), Brammer said that his team’s incredibly hard work has earned them a clean month off. “Everyone has done something that’s not in their job description,” he told the magazine. “Everyone wears multiple hats, because they can see the path to success with the product.”

As for players, who are somewhat turning on the game with its recent (40,000) reviews averaging to “Mixed” with only 51 percent being positive, they too can take December to themselves the CEO suggests. “We’re going to make sure players know they can fuck off in December,” Brammer said. “Go playÂ GTA 6, we need to detox.”

Robot lamp maker Syncere raises $11M pre-seed led by a16z Speedrun

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The $2,699 Lume is a floor lamp that folds laundry, and every unit shipped trains the company’s AI.

The Lume robot lamp handles household chores.

Syncere, the Palo Alto startup behind the Lume robot lamp, has raised $11m in pre-seed funding led by a16z Speedrun, Andreessen Horowitz’s programme for early-stage founders.

Draper Associates, Physical World, Sequel and SeaX Ventures also took part in the round. The company plans to use the money to deliver its first Lume units, expand manufacturing capacity and adapt its robotics technology for commercial applications.

Lume combines a sculptural floor lamp with a mobile home robot. During the day, it serves as a light. It can also move around the house to handle everyday chores, including folding laundry, tidying rooms, and making beds.

The lamp starts at $2,699, and owners can teach it how they want specific tasks done, while Syncere is developing an onboarding process that will help it learn household preferences and adapt to the layout of each home.

“Home robotics has a deployment problem disguised as a data problem,” said Aaron Tan, Syncere’s co-founder and chief executive. “Robots need real-world data to become reliable, but they can’t get into people’s homes until they’re reliable.”

Lume is designed to break that cycle. By selling the robot as a household light first, Syncere hopes to get its machines into homes earlier, gather data from real-world use, and improve their capabilities through software updates.

The company says customers should see their robots become more capable over time without having to replace the hardware.

Robotics researchers Aaron Tan and Angus Fung founded Syncere less than a year ago. Since then, the company says, Lume has moved from concept to production and into customers’ homes.

The founding team’s backgrounds include Stanford, Harvard, the University of Toronto and UC Berkeley, alongside engineering and design experience at Nuro, AMD, GM and Apple.

Interest has already built ahead of the first deliveries. TIME named Lume one of its Best Inventions of 2026, and Syncere says it has secured millions of dollars in pre-orders.

“Getting robots into real homes earlier creates a fundamentally different path to improving household autonomy,” said Andrew Chen, general partner at a16z Speedrun.

Chen said Syncere stood out because it had designed its sales channel and learning system together, while demonstrating demand before shipping its product.

The home robotics market is attracting a growing number of companies. Norway’s 1X opened orders earlier this year for NEO, a $20,000 humanoid designed to handle household chores, while other manufacturers are developing cheaper machines for specific tasks.

Syncere is taking a different route, betting that a robot designed to look like furniture will be easier to bring into people’s homes.

Stronger US factory activity complicates Bitcoin’s hopes for lower rates

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

ISM’s production index reached 59 as factory price increases became less widespread, leaving growth and funding signals in tension.

U.S. manufacturers’ production gauge reached its highest reading since November 2021 in October, giving Bitcoin investors a stronger growth signal alongside persistent factory cost pressure. The combination could complicate the case for easier U.S. interest rates even as firmer activity could support appetite for risk.

The Institute for Supply Management’s report released November 2 put its production index at 59, up from September’s 56.7. The headline manufacturing PMI rose to 55.5 from 54.5, marking a tenth consecutive month of expansion in the survey.

The prices index moved in the opposite direction, falling to 75.2 from 77.9. That is a meaningful counterweight to the stronger production reading: raw-material price increases became less widespread. Yet October still marked the 25th consecutive month of rising raw-material prices.

These are diffusion indexes, which measure the breadth of monthly changes across respondents. The production milestone concerns a survey reading, not the physical level of factory output. Likewise, the prices index measures how widespread increases are, rather than the inflation rate.

ISM’s October production index rose as its prices index eased. The combination leaves Bitcoin with competing growth and funding signals.

Stronger activity could support risk appetite. Continued cost increases could constrain rate relief; neither channel establishes Bitcoin’s price response.

The production milestone refers to an index, not physical output. The prices index fell while raw-material prices still rose. Bitcoin channels are analytical possibilities, not measured effects. The Fed held rates; two dissents did not enact a hike.

Reported September and October index levels share a linear, zoomed 50–80 scale; y=340−8×(index−50). The dots show survey indexes, not output or inflation rates. Connector geometry is qualitative, with no implied effect size or forecast.

The production milestone refers to an index, not physical output. The prices index fell while raw-material prices still rose. Bitcoin channels are analytical possibilities, not measured effects. The Fed held rates; two dissents did not enact a hike.

The Federal Reserve held its target range at 3.75%–4% on October 28, citing strong economic activity and elevated inflation. The decision passed 10–2, with Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack preferring a quarter-point increase.

A 2023 IMF working paper found that Fed tightening reduces a common crypto-price factor through the risk-taking channel, meaning changes in investors’ willingness to hold risky assets. That historical result helps explain why the rate outlook matters for crypto.

For the rates outlook, the next distinction is whether investors expect borrowing costs to stay high. Firmer expectations for short-term rates would reinforce the restrictive-funding interpretation; falling yields alongside resilient Bitcoin demand would weaken it.

Snowflake expands agentic AI platform with business context and governance tools

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Snowflake Inc. today announced a sweeping collection of artificial intelligence capabilities designed to bring business context, governance and autonomous development into the same environment, as enterprises increasingly hand over software engineering and everyday operations to AI agents.

The announcements, during the company’s Expedition 2026 virtual conference, include a new Business Ontology framework, expanded agent governance through Cortex AI Gateway, and autonomous data engineering capabilities that allow AI agents to build, monitor and troubleshoot data pipelines with less human intervention.

The company is betting that more coders and engineers will use AI throughout the software development lifecycle as creation moves even faster, while other back-end departments begin to adopt agents in their day-to-day work. For example, sales operations are creating agents that can analyze sales risks and prepare customer outreach without writing code.

“AI is industrializing software creation. Actually, it’s industrializing intelligence,” Chief Executive Sridhar Ramaswamy said. “What once took months can now take hours, days, or even minutes.”

Ramaswamy noted that while AI is changing the economics of software development, as agents take over tasks usually relegated to specialized developers, business users become developers. This is changing the competitive landscape, meaning that the advantage no longer goes to specialists, but to proprietary knowledge.

Snowflake is taking the field by helping ordinary AI models understand and contextualize that business knowledge. It does this with Business Ontology, an extension of its existing semantic views that lets organizations establish approved definitions used across the organization. Without a standard definition, two agents could respond to the same business-related question with incompatible answers, even when both agents have accurate underlying data.

Using information from Business Ontology, Snowflake CoCo, the coding agent for data teams, and Snowflake CoWork, the personal work agent for knowledge workers, use these approved business concepts to ground their responses.

This new feature is currently in private preview, but is headed to public preview soon.

Cortex AI Gateway arrives to become a single front door for agents within an organization. It is not entirely new; Snowflake introduced it in July 2026 as a centralized way to govern and monitor agents.

Today’s announcement expands its capabilities rather than introducing the gateway for the first time. The new updates include agent abuse detection, observability and tracing, Cortex sandboxes, AI cost governance, dynamic model routing and tools integration using technology from Snowflake’s Natoma acquisition.

“An agent that acts on your business has to be trusted, and it has to know three important things: what the data means, which data to trust, and what it’s allowed to do,” said Ramaswamy.

The platform most importantly applies existing security mechanisms, including data masking and access restrictions, to agent-driven operations while adding capabilities to monitor activity, detect potential abuse and maintain records of what agents do. The information technology and security teams can also apply cost controls, select models based on efficiency or accuracy and restrict which external tools agents can access granularly.

The underlying role of this governance, control and transparency is aligned with giving agents the same sort of role observation granted to employees. Treating them similarly in terms of autonomy.

Snowflake’s push comes amid a broader industry race to establish the control layer for enterprise AI agents. SAP has been expanding its Autonomous Enterprise architecture and governed Joule agents, while Salesforce has introduced its Enterprise AI Harness and AI Control Plane to secure autonomous workflows. Snowflake is approaching the same problem from the data platform side, seeking to become the foundation through which agents access trusted business information and operate under enterprise policies.

Snowflake also announced the public preview of Cortex Training, which will allow customers to tailor open-weight AI models with their own proprietary information.

The service is designed to make it simpler to create specialized models using post-training without needing vast arrays of graphics processing units. Snowflake lets them train models without managing the underlying infrastructure.

“Specialized, smaller models that come with more efficient responses, faster responses, and frequently cost benefits in terms of tokens,” Executive Vice President of Product Christian Kleinerman said.

Many enterprise companies will require sovereign control of their own AI models as increasing restrictions on foreign open-weight AI or regulations on handling sensitive information. It’s easier to audit what passes through an AI model on your own hardware than in a third-party vendor’s cloud.

Separately, Snowflake announced an expanded partnership with Oracle Corp. to make its platform available as a first-party service on Oracle Cloud Infrastructure. The integration will give customers easier access to Snowflake’s analytics and AI capabilities while allowing them to work directly through Oracle databases and applications.

Groundcover turns its Wand acquisition into a Kubernetes rightsizing beta

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Observability company groundcover Inc. today added Autonomous Infrastructure to its platform in beta to rightsize the processor and memory requests automatically on customers’ Kubernetes workloads based on how each workload behaves in production.

The technology comes from Kubernetes resource optimization startup Wand Cloud Ltd., which groundcover acquired in September.

The release is aimed at the processor and memory requests Kubernetes has engineers set for each workload up front, usually before it has handled any real traffic, and groundcover said most teams seldom return to those numbers. When one is set too low, the first sign is often a service throwing out-of-memory errors. Padding that goes unused ends up on the monthly cloud bill.

Because the groundcover sensor has been collecting production history all along, a customer on a supported version can open Autonomous Infrastructure for the first time without installing anything and find the data the feature needs already in place. Requested processor and memory for each workload appear next to actual usage. Estimated monthly savings sit beside a count of under-provisioned workloads, and a chart tracks over time how much of the potential saving has been realized.

Dollar figures in the beta are based on a price per processor and memory unit that customers enter themselves. Public cloud pricing is the next step, according to groundcover, and billing data such as the Cost and Usage Report from Amazon Web Services Inc. will come after that.

Most resource optimization tools size each workload in isolation, groundcover argues, so a request that looks right for one service can still starve its neighbors on a crowded node. Stability is the first constraint in groundcover’s own software. Before Autonomous Infrastructure raises a request, it checks how tightly the node is already packed so one workload’s increase does not leave the rest short of resources.

Shahar Azulay, groundcover’s co-founder and chief executive, said customers can check what their clusters stand to save, from data already held in their own cloud accounts, before they let Autonomous Infrastructure “change anything.” The feature stays off until an engineer turns it on and picks the deployments, namespaces or clusters it manages.

During the beta the software touches requests only and leaves limits where they are. Each policy carries minimum and maximum bounds for processor and memory, and an audit log records who assigned each workload to a policy and when. Groundcover suggests starting with lower-risk workloads such as development namespaces and leaving alone something like a production database that needs room to grow.

The beta is available now at no additional cost to every groundcover customer running its sensor on Kubernetes, and the company is also recruiting design partners to work with its product team while it runs. Demonstrations are scheduled for KubeCon + CloudNativeCon North America in Salt Lake City Nov. 9-12.

Groundcover is a venture capital-backed startup that has raised $160 million to date, including a $100 million Series C round led by One Peak in July.
